===== BEGIN SOURCE docs/v12-platform.md | original-bytes=19980 | sha256=0b1b3ea41f8bbb280ed11d3c3cb3428a437518b9c502a887fbd8a6079ec981f6 =====
# MCP Device 1.0.12 platform guide

This source extends the existing MCP Device package. `start` remains the Device-only runtime; `device` is an alias for the existing Device command family. `server` starts the account/OAuth control plane and one mandatory local Device runtime using the same transport-neutral Device Core as remote Devices.

The acceptance record is [Spec 008](../specs/008-v12-platform/ACCEPTANCE.md). A built source tree, a locally tested tarball, npm publication and a production deployment are separate states. This increment does not publish or deploy the package.

## Runtime and state

Node 22.13 or newer is required, including built-in SQLite/FTS5. Acceptance uses the exact Node versions recorded in Spec 008. Native Device handlers retain their SDK v1 dependency; the retained control-plane surface uses SDK v2. The dependency lock is part of the candidate.

```text
mcp-device start
mcp-device device start
mcp-device device status
mcp-device server --runtime-dir <server-state> --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8104
# Trusted-LAN HTTP example with one shared server Skill root:
mcp-device server --runtime-dir <server-state> --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8101 --public-url http://abc:8101 --allow-http-lan --skill-root ~/.mcp-device/skills
mcp-device --help
```

Server state defaults to `~/.mcp-device/server`; legacy Device state defaults remain `~/.mcp-device`. An explicit `--runtime-dir` selects platform command state. `MCP_DEVICE_CONFIG_DIR` is also accepted by platform commands and the native execution configuration; it must be set before native modules load. Legacy Device identity and service configuration retain their existing path rules. Server starts the native modules only after selecting its state root. `--skill-root DIR` selects the single server-global Skill catalog shared by authenticated accounts; when omitted, an existing `~/.mcp-device/skills` directory is preferred for legacy/shared-team compatibility, otherwise the bundled distribution Skills are used. `MCP_SKILL_ROOT` is the service-friendly equivalent.

`--allow-http-lan` is an explicit trusted-LAN opt-in. It requires an explicit `--public-url http://...` and permits that non-loopback HTTP URL to be used as the OAuth issuer/resource base. Without the flag, non-loopback OAuth issuers remain HTTPS-only. This changes transport policy only; account authentication, Device ownership, pairing and cross-account isolation remain unchanged.

TODO: external MCP upstreams are currently server-global. Before relying on upstreams in multi-account deployments, add per-upstream ownership (`owner_account_id`) with private-by-default visibility and explicit sharing. Shared server Skills remain intentionally cross-account.

Server is a foreground process. Stop it with Ctrl-C or the supervising process manager. A Node parent using a private IPC channel may send `{ "type": "mcp-device:shutdown" }` for graceful shutdown on Windows. This is a parent/child process channel, not a network endpoint.

The state root contains identity, pairing/account state, the Gateway SQLite database, plugin registry/private data, a persisted knowledge-reference signing key and bounded activity records. Concurrent server, Device and maintenance processes cannot take the same state lease. Existing Device RuntimeOwner IPC is also checked. An unresponsive legacy control file requires inspection; maintenance does not silently stop another runtime or discard the control state.

The local execution owner does not connect to its own WebSocket or spawn another Device MCP server. A Palace worker process is plugin isolation, not a second Device runtime. Remote Devices continue using their authenticated GatewayDeviceChannel and reconnect/session-epoch contracts.

## Account, OAuth and pairing

Localhost follows the ordinary account, OAuth and Device pairing flows. Anonymous MCP calls are rejected. Create an initial administrator using the existing administrative command, supplying the password through `HCU_ADMIN_PASSWORD` rather than command-line arguments:

```text
mcp-device admin --runtime-dir <server-state> account create-admin <email>
mcp-device admin --runtime-dir <server-state> invite create 1
mcp-device server --runtime-dir <server-state> --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8104
```

The administrator manages invitations and the operator interface. Use an invitation to create an ordinary account at `/signup`; Device pairing and OAuth consent use that ordinary account, preserving the existing role separation.

The server prints one JSON event per line for `device_pairing_required` and `server_ready`. The pairing event contains the public user code and verification URL; it excludes the private device code and PKCE verifier. Open the verification URL, sign in with the ordinary account and claim the local Device. MCP clients then use the standard registration/authorization/consent/token flow. A restored identity and matching server state retain enrollment, accounts and refresh-token continuity.

The operator UI is under `/operator` on the same listener and requires administrator authentication. `/operator/state` uses the same policy. There is no automatic second operator port.

For remote Devices, provide the existing Gateway application TLS configuration through a server JSON config. `--config` accepts an object with an optional `env` object and an optional `configPath` for upstream configuration. `configPath` resolves relative to that JSON file. Configuration names and certificate requirements remain the retained Gateway contracts; incomplete TLS configuration fails startup and closes acquired resources. The isolated localhost test configuration does not provision production trust or bypass remote Device TLS requirements.

```text
mcp-device server --runtime-dir <server-state> --config <server-config.json> --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8104
```

Upstream configuration is selected consistently for both workspace and external MCP manager startup. Explicit options take precedence over the runtime-root configuration and bundled fallback. Existing upstream tools/resources/prompts, cache/reconciliation and collision rules remain in the control-plane adapter.

## Plugin lifecycle

Palace is supplied by the official distribution and initially installed and enabled. The Device Core has no Palace dependency. A disabled, uninstalled, crashed or incompatible provider loses its advertised capabilities while native file/process tools remain available. Uninstall leaves a tombstone, so later starts do not silently reinstall the plugin. Disable and uninstall preserve private data; purge is a separate explicit command for a disabled plugin.

```text
mcp-device plugins --runtime-dir <state> list
mcp-device plugins --runtime-dir <state> disable palace
mcp-device plugins --runtime-dir <state> enable palace
mcp-device plugins --runtime-dir <state> uninstall palace
mcp-device plugins --runtime-dir <state> install <manifest.json> --enable
mcp-device plugins --runtime-dir <state> configure <plugin-id> <settings.json>
mcp-device plugins --runtime-dir <state> upgrade <manifest.json>
mcp-device plugins --runtime-dir <state> upgrade palace --bundled
mcp-device plugins --runtime-dir <state> purge <plugin-id>
```

Configure requires a disabled plugin. Upgrade and purge require stopped runtime ownership so that migration/deletion cannot race a live provider. Upgrade works on a private copy and restores the previous manifest/data if validation or migration fails. An external manifest becomes a user-managed (`manual`) registration and is not reclaimed by the bundled distribution. `--bundled` explicitly restores the distribution source. A version-only distribution update with the same capabilities and data schema is re-pinned without a data migration; capability or schema changes remain gated behind explicit upgrade. Registry writes use revision checks, a tokenized exclusive lock and a stale-lock recovery lock; another runtime sees enabled-capability changes through refresh notifications.

The host is capability-oriented: providers, ordered augmenters, observers and fallback providers compose through versioned contracts. Dependencies resolve to healthy compatible providers and are pinned during an activation. A plugin can invoke its declared dependencies through scoped host services. Dependency loss invalidates dependent providers. Faults are sticky across startup, refresh and dependency activation: none of these automatically restarts a faulted provider. After inspecting the recorded error, explicitly run `mcp-device plugins --runtime-dir <state> enable <plugin-id>` to retry; faulted dependents also require explicit enable after their provider recovers. Missing-entry/bundled upgrades require a stopped runtime; an agent reports that prerequisite and must not stop the runtime itself. Process isolation supports Node workers and external JSONL executables; explicitly trusted cooperative plugins may use the in-process path. See the [plugin contract](../specs/008-v12-platform/plugin-contract.md) for the API, cancellation, frame limits and examples.

Public MCP `capability.list` reports an owned Device's available capabilities and versions. `capability.call` invokes an advertised non-knowledge plugin contract through the normal authenticated Device dispatcher. It does not expose private knowledge import; knowledge exchange uses the three public contracts below.

## Knowledge federation

Knowledge exchange is a platform service. A requesting Device can use it without Palace or any local knowledge database.

| Public tool | Inputs | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `knowledge.search` | `device_id`, `query`; optional `project_id`, `limit`, `max_bytes`, `provider_device_id` | Select relevant available providers and return compact signed references. No import. |
| `knowledge.list` | `device_id`; optional scope/provider/budget fields | Return compact scoped references. No import. |
| `knowledge.read` | `device_id`, `ref`; optional `project_id`, `evidence`, `max_bytes` | Read one selected card. Lazily import only when the requesting Device supplies the compatible storage capability. |

Requests use authenticated account context supplied by the control plane. The caller cannot select another account through tool arguments. A Device has one current owner account, and the broker rejects ownerless or cross-account dispatch before invoking its Core. References bind the account, selected provider, origin, card version and project scope. Local-only cards do not cross Device boundaries. Provider results carry the authenticated serving Device identity rather than trusting a self-reported identity field. Palace SQLite is Device-local private state and may exist before pairing; a deliberate signed ownership transfer transfers that local state with the Device, while the old account immediately loses dispatch and federation access.

The router prefers local/project-relevant providers and uses a bounded provider count. It does not broadcast to every Device, replicate whole knowledge stores or start background pairwise synchronization. Search/list return previews. Read returns a complete card within the response budget or an explicit budget error; it never truncates a card while keeping a hash for the full content.

For a Device with a storage provider, repeated reads preserve origin, evidence, provenance, version and deduplication identity. For a Device without one, the platform uses a bounded session cache (256 entries, 8 MiB, five-minute TTL). It creates no Palace database on that Device.

Read performs an on-demand origin metadata check when available. An unavailable origin can leave an unchanged cached card explicitly stale. A known correction, withdrawal, scope revocation or version inconsistency must not silently fall back to an older usable card. Correction guards are bounded to the session; this is not a globally synchronized revocation database. Persisted card revisions and later online origin checks remain authoritative.

## Palace CLI, package and Skill lifecycle

The MCP Device package keeps the stable `palace` command required by the CLI-first contract. The independent `@hcu-lab.me/palace` package exposes the same command and canonical compiled implementation when installed in a standalone or separate npm prefix. Installing both root packages into the same global prefix is unsupported because npm cannot give one executable name two owners; npm must fail with `EEXIST`, and `--force` is unsupported. The standalone package includes `LICENSE`, `palace.plugin.json`, its Skill installer and a closed import graph. A manifest installed or upgraded from that package is recorded as a manual PluginHost source.

Palace Skill files are user-home package assets, not per-runtime plugin state. A per-user ownership ledger records every package owner and the installed baseline hashes. Installation uses plain copied files by default, stages replacements outside agent discovery roots, publishes by rename, and recovers recorded interrupted updates. A pristine managed copy can upgrade or relocate; a modified or foreign same-name Skill is preserved. The final pristine owner may remove the managed Skill, while another installed owner keeps it available. Ordinary `palace` reads are metadata-only and never reconcile Skill files. If lifecycle scripts were skipped, `palace status`/`palace skill status` reports the missing Skill and the exact recovery command `palace skill install`; starting the Device/server runtime also performs a non-blocking reconciliation. Explicit inspection and repair are available through `palace skill status|install|repair|remove|adopt|prune`.

Every agent workflow starts with `palace status`. The response identifies the package, version, install path, runtime root, plugin state and write policy. Without an explicit root, the CLI and `mcp-device plugins|knowledge` use the same resolver: explicit argument, then `MCP_DEVICE_CONFIG_DIR`, then active lease, then a single Palace registry; ambiguous roots fail typed instead of guessing. Disabled, uninstalled, faulted, upgrade-in-progress or upgrade-required runtimes return typed actionable limitations. Lifecycle status is emitted as structured JSON on stdout even when the exit code is 1; malformed arguments, unsafe paths and SQLite/open failures are structured JSON on stderr. Palace knowledge operations are read-only. `palace skill install|repair|remove|adopt|prune` mutate only the user-home Skill ownership state. When a Device runtime is stopped, knowledge mutation uses `mcp-device knowledge --runtime-dir <state> ...` under the exclusive server lease; a standalone Palace installation without `mcp-device` has no mutation path. The Skill never stops the runtime by itself.

## Palace construction and learning

Palace builds durable knowledge cards and procedural candidates from local source evidence. It is not a replacement chat-history table. Its bounded offline discovery includes recognized Pi, Claude, Codex and MCP/Palace locations plus explicitly configured projects, logs, Skills and Git evidence. Source discovery/indexing requires no model and no cloud connection. Original sources are read, not rewritten. Missing or changed sources are detected when exact evidence is read.

Default background indexing is bounded; settings can disable it with `autoIndex: false` or adjust `indexBudget` (`maxRecords`, `maxFiles`, `maxBytes`, `intervalMs`). The interval is clamped between one second and one hour, with a 30-second default. `--project-root` and `--source-root` add explicit roots. Existing P1 `logs` and gated Context commands keep their own behavior.

The platform activity journal records bounded execution metadata and real exit/status information. It does not store full commands, file contents or raw stdout and does not treat an activity row as proof of successful task completion. Rotated or missing source evidence is not fabricated.

Recurring explicit fixes from independent sources can produce an inactive procedure candidate automatically. Activating a learned Skill requires trusted local validation: actual execution receipts plus an independent postcondition. Caller-supplied `success` fields or an invented validation result are insufficient. Automatic activation is opt-in (`autoActivateSkills`) and needs a trusted local `validationModule` exporting the expected runner factory. No automated edit to `AGENTS.md` is performed.

The local knowledge CLI is useful with a stopped runtime, including before account pairing:

```text
mcp-device knowledge --runtime-dir <state> search "{\"query\":\"reconnect timeout\"}"
mcp-device knowledge --runtime-dir <state> list
mcp-device knowledge --runtime-dir <state> read @<request.json>
mcp-device knowledge --runtime-dir <state> ingest @<request.json>
mcp-device knowledge --runtime-dir <state> learn @<request.json>
mcp-device knowledge --runtime-dir <state> correct @<request.json>
mcp-device knowledge --runtime-dir <state> skills
```

Use a JSON request file when shell quoting is inconvenient. For an active runtime, use its authenticated MCP surface. See [Palace design and measured limitations](../specs/008-v12-platform/palace-design.md) for card/evidence contracts and benchmark interpretation. The initial retriever is lexical; semantic/vector/graph expansion is not enabled without evidence that it helps.

## Backup, migration and rollback

Operate on actual runtime state directories, not source checkouts. Stop the runtime before copying SQLite state. A nonempty WAL/journal is rejected until shutdown/checkpoint has completed. Backup does not reset identity, passwords, accounts, invitations or plugin data.

```text
mcp-device state backup --source <stopped-state> --backup <new-backup-dir>
mcp-device state verify --backup <backup-dir>
mcp-device state restore --backup <backup-dir> --target <new-state-dir>
mcp-device state migrate --device-root <old-device-state> --gateway-root <old-gateway-state> --target <new-state-dir> --backup <new-backup-dir>
mcp-device state migrate --device-root <old-device-state> --gateway-root <old-gateway-state> --target <new-state-dir> --backup <new-backup-dir> --apply
```

Migration defaults to a dry-run plan. Apply first creates and verifies separate backups, then copies into a new target and validates its file hashes. Existing targets, overlapping roots, symlinks/junctions, unsafe paths and collisions are rejected. The original roots remain available for rollback; restore publishes only a new verified target. Plugin private files and managed evidence are included. Externally referenced original source files remain external; a backup cannot manufacture missing source evidence.

See [state migration contract](../specs/008-v12-platform/state-migration.md) for the manifest, collision policy and recovery sequence. Package relocation and plugin upgrade are tested separately from byte-exact state restoration; preserve user-managed plugin code or supply its current manifest when moving hosts.

## Reproducing source acceptance

Use an isolated checkout and explicit test state. The repository provides executable inventories and independent baseline/candidate parity runners.

```text
npm ci
npm run build
npm run test:v12
npm run test:parity -- --list --group port-free
npm run test:parity -- --group port-free --output <isolated-evidence-dir>
npm run test:v12:server
```

Server acceptance owns port 8104 for its duration. The cold-package test additionally requires `MCP_V12_COLD_PACKAGE_ROOT` pointing at the package inside an isolated production-dependency npm installation. It verifies the installed entry point, default enabled Palace, normal pairing/OAuth, native execution, backup/restore, retained identity and refresh-token continuity.

On the current G6 test machine, port 8103 belongs to an existing audit process and is preserved. G8 ports 8102 (production) and 8101 (backup) are outside these tests. No production rollout is authorized by source acceptance.

===== BEGIN SOURCE docs/v12-tasks-123.md | original-bytes=3464 | sha256=30e5c23f3bdd32014e32514e4b964130a30d62206683eec17e2b1210fcb217d1 =====
# MCP Device v12 task 1–3: runtime and Palace CLI

## Task 1: retire NeuralMemory from MCP, not from disk

Production config `/home/ayumi/.mcp-gateway-8102/runtime/mcp-v12-no-nmem.toml` disables the external MCP manager and deletes the `nmem` upstream/always-on memory instructions. The old config, NeuralMemory service, and its data are retained to avoid breaking unrelated consumers. The server must no longer expose `nmem_*` tools. The Palace plugin is independent of NeuralMemory.

## Task 2: one authenticated backend, two local ports

Run `mcp-device server ... --port 8102 --loopback-port 8101 --public-url https://device.hcu-lab.me`.

- Public Cloudflare ingress stays `https://device.hcu-lab.me/mcp` -> `127.0.0.1:8102/mcp`.
- G8 local agent uses `http://127.0.0.1:8101/mcp`; the port is loopback-bound, rejects proxied/foreign Host requests and **requires the same account auth**.
- Both listeners share one Express application, database, Device broker, embedded Core, and plugin process; there is no second server state or device identity.
- Stop old `mcp-v11-8101` to free the local port. Cloudflare's old `mcp.hcu-lab.me` hostname is not an approved alternative to the new public origin and should be denied by the loopback Host guard.
- Existing remote Device identities remain untouched. Cutover must be rollback-capable with stopped-state snapshot.

## Task 3: CLI-first Palace as an installable plugin

Palace is an optional removable process-isolated plugin, delivered enabled by default. It does not require a Palace MCP server.

```bash
palace --runtime-dir /path/to/server/runtime status
palace --runtime-dir /path/to/server/runtime search "a specific failure" --limit 3
palace --runtime-dir /path/to/server/runtime read CARD_ID
palace --runtime-dir /path/to/server/runtime skills
```

The CLI uses a **read-only SQLite connection** so it can query the active Palace WAL without trying to own the server state lease or become another writer. Snapshot reads report `evidenceVerifiedNow: false`, not a false provenance guarantee. Write-side ingest/validation/correction remains owned by the running plugin.

The reusable Skill is at `skills/palace-cli/SKILL.md`; agents must load it only when relevant. The default CLI server does **not** advertise Palace `knowledge.*` MCP tools. `--legacy-knowledge-mcp` exists solely for compatibility/older regression fixtures. Generic MCP Device tools and the installable plugin lifecycle remain independent.

Current CLI binary is shipped with the MCP Device distribution under `dist/plugins/palace/cli.js`, with a `palace` command alias. Separating the plugin into a separately versioned, standalone npm package is follow-up packaging work; do not claim it is completed yet.

## Quality and token-economics release gate

A large indexed card count is only an indexing metric. Claim real improvement **only** after held-out end-to-end tasks under a fixed model/tool budget compare:

- no-Palace baseline vs Skill+Palace CLI;
- task correctness verified by independent postconditions;
- repeated-error rate and abstention/stale recommendation rate;
- actual model input/output/cached tokens and tool-result bytes;
- first-task/validation overhead amortized over repeated tasks;
- p50/p95 latency, task elapsed time and total cost.

Retained fixture replay reduced retries but increased agent-visible bytes; that does **not** prove token savings in production.

===== BEGIN SOURCE scripts/analyze-fuzzy-logs.js | original-bytes=8736 | sha256=5f27956a5afad9d1f348ed84b40664d0128d4917a9df2a4f7ad9e0e66227a67f =====
#!/usr/bin/env node

import { fuzzySearchLogger } from '../dist/utils/fuzzySearchLogger.js';

// Simple argument parsing
const args = process.argv.slice(2);
let failureThreshold = 0.7;
let limit = 100;

// Parse arguments
for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i++) {
  if (args[i] === '--threshold' || args[i] === '-t') {
    failureThreshold = parseFloat(args[i + 1]) || 0.7;
  }
  if (args[i] === '--limit' || args[i] === '-l') {
    limit = parseInt(args[i + 1], 10) || 100;
  }
  if (args[i].startsWith('--threshold=')) {
    failureThreshold = parseFloat(args[i].split('=')[1]) || 0.7;
  }
  if (args[i].startsWith('--limit=')) {
    limit = parseInt(args[i].split('=')[1], 10) || 100;
  }
}

if (args.includes('--help') || args.includes('-h')) {
  console.log(`Analyze fuzzy search logs for patterns and issues

Usage: node analyze-fuzzy-logs.js [options]

Options:
  -t, --threshold <number>  Failure threshold (0-1) (default: 0.7)
  -l, --limit <number>      Maximum number of logs to analyze (default: 100)
  -h, --help               Show this help message`);
  process.exit(0);
}

async function analyzeLogs() {
  try {
    const logs = await fuzzySearchLogger.getRecentLogs(limit);
    const logPath = await fuzzySearchLogger.getLogPath();
    
    if (logs.length === 0) {
      console.log(`No fuzzy search logs found. Log file location: ${logPath}`);
      return;
    }
    
    console.log('\n=== Fuzzy Search Analysis ===\n');
    
    // Parse logs and gather statistics
    let totalEntries = 0;
    let exactMatches = 0;
    let fuzzyMatches = 0;
    let failures = 0;
    let belowThresholdCount = 0;
    const executionTimes = [];
    const similarities = [];
    const fileExtensions = new Map();
    const commonCharacterCodes = new Map();
    const failureReasons = [];
    
    for (const log of logs) {
      const parts = log.split('\t');
      if (parts.length >= 16) {
        totalEntries++;
        const [
          timestamp, searchText, foundText, similarity, 
          executionTime, exactMatchCount, expectedReplacements,
          fuzzyThreshold, belowThreshold, diff,
          searchLength, foundLength, fileExtension,
          characterCodes, uniqueCharacterCount, diffLength
        ] = parts;
        
        const simValue = parseFloat(similarity);
        const execTime = parseFloat(executionTime);
        const exactCount = parseInt(exactMatchCount);
        const belowThresh = belowThreshold === 'true';
        
        if (exactCount > 0) {
          exactMatches++;
        } else if (simValue >= failureThreshold) {
          fuzzyMatches++;
        } else {
          failures++;
          // Store failure case for analysis
          failureReasons.push({
            similarity: simValue,
            diff: diff.replace(/\\n/g, '\n').replace(/\\t/g, '\t'),
            fileExtension,
            characterCodes
          });
        }
        
        if (belowThresh) {
          belowThresholdCount++;
        }
        
        executionTimes.push(execTime);
        similarities.push(simValue);
        
        // Track file extensions
        fileExtensions.set(fileExtension, (fileExtensions.get(fileExtension) || 0) + 1);
        
        // Track character codes that appear in diffs
        if (characterCodes && characterCodes !== '') {
          const codes = characterCodes.split(',');
          for (const code of codes) {
            const key = code.split(':')[0];
            commonCharacterCodes.set(key, (commonCharacterCodes.get(key) || 0) + 1);
          }
        }
      }
    }
    
    // Calculate statistics
    const avgExecutionTime = executionTimes.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / executionTimes.length;
    const avgSimilarity = similarities.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / similarities.length;
    const maxExecutionTime = Math.max(...executionTimes);
    const minExecutionTime = Math.min(...executionTimes);
    
    // Sort by frequency
    const sortedExtensions = Array.from(fileExtensions.entries()).sort((a, b) => b[1] - a[1]);
    const sortedCharCodes = Array.from(commonCharacterCodes.entries()).sort((a, b) => b[1] - a[1]);
    
    // Display results
    console.log(`Total Entries: ${totalEntries}`);
    console.log(`Exact Matches: ${exactMatches} (${((exactMatches / totalEntries) * 100).toFixed(2)}%)`);
    console.log(`Fuzzy Matches: ${fuzzyMatches} (${((fuzzyMatches / totalEntries) * 100).toFixed(2)}%)`);
    console.log(`Failures: ${failures} (${((failures / totalEntries) * 100).toFixed(2)}%)`);
    console.log(`Below Threshold: ${belowThresholdCount} (${((belowThresholdCount / totalEntries) * 100).toFixed(2)}%)`);
    
    console.log('\n--- Performance Metrics ---');
    console.log(`Average Execution Time: ${avgExecutionTime.toFixed(2)}ms`);
    console.log(`Min Execution Time: ${minExecutionTime.toFixed(2)}ms`);
    console.log(`Max Execution Time: ${maxExecutionTime.toFixed(2)}ms`);
    console.log(`Average Similarity: ${(avgSimilarity * 100).toFixed(2)}%`);
    
    console.log('\n--- File Extensions (Top 5) ---');
    sortedExtensions.slice(0, 5).forEach(([ext, count]) => {
      console.log(`${ext || 'none'}: ${count} times`);
    });
    
    console.log('\n--- Common Character Codes in Diffs (Top 10) ---');
    sortedCharCodes.slice(0, 10).forEach(([code, count]) => {
      const charCode = parseInt(code);
      const char = String.fromCharCode(charCode);
      const display = charCode < 32 || charCode > 126 ? `\\x${charCode.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')}` : char;
      console.log(`${code} [${display}]: ${count} times`);
    });
    
    // Analyze failure patterns
    if (failures > 0) {
      console.log('\n--- Failure Analysis ---');
      console.log(`Total failures: ${failures}`);
      
      // Group failures by similarity ranges
      const similarityRanges = {
        '0-20%': 0,
        '21-40%': 0,
        '41-60%': 0,
        '61-80%': 0,
        '81-99%': 0
      };
      
      failureReasons.forEach(failure => {
        const sim = failure.similarity * 100;
        if (sim <= 20) similarityRanges['0-20%']++;
        else if (sim <= 40) similarityRanges['21-40%']++;
        else if (sim <= 60) similarityRanges['41-60%']++;
        else if (sim <= 80) similarityRanges['61-80%']++;
        else similarityRanges['81-99%']++;
      });
      
      console.log('\nFailures by similarity range:');
      Object.entries(similarityRanges).forEach(([range, count]) => {
        if (count > 0) {
          console.log(`  ${range}: ${count} failures`);
        }
      });
      
      // Show most common failure reasons
      const failuresByExtension = new Map();
      failureReasons.forEach(failure => {
        const key = failure.fileExtension || 'none';
        failuresByExtension.set(key, (failuresByExtension.get(key) || 0) + 1);
      });
      
      console.log('\nFailures by file extension:');
      Array.from(failuresByExtension.entries())
        .sort((a, b) => b[1] - a[1])
        .slice(0, 5)
        .forEach(([ext, count]) => {
          console.log(`  ${ext}: ${count} failures`);
        });
    }
    
    // Recommendations
    console.log('\n--- Recommendations ---');
    if (failures > totalEntries * 0.1) {
      console.log(`⚠️  High failure rate (${((failures / totalEntries) * 100).toFixed(1)}%). Consider:
  - Reviewing search text formatting (whitespace, line endings)
  - Checking for encoding issues
  - Using smaller, more specific search patterns`);
    }
    
    if (avgExecutionTime > 100) {
      console.log(`⚠️  Slow execution times (avg: ${avgExecutionTime.toFixed(2)}ms). Consider:
  - Reducing search text length
  - Breaking large edits into smaller chunks`);
    }
    
    if (sortedCharCodes.length > 0) {
      const topCharCode = sortedCharCodes[0];
      const charCode = parseInt(topCharCode[0]);
      if (charCode === 13 || charCode === 10) {
        console.log(`💡 Most common character differences involve line endings (CR/LF).
  Consider normalizing line endings in your search text.`);
      } else if (charCode === 32 || charCode === 9) {
        console.log(`💡 Most common character differences involve whitespace.
  Consider trimming whitespace or being more specific about spacing.`);
      }
    }
    
    console.log(`\nLog file location: ${logPath}`);
    console.log(`Analysis completed for ${totalEntries} entries.`);
    
  } catch (error) {
    console.error('Failed to analyze fuzzy search logs:', error.message);
    process.exit(1);
  }
}

analyzeLogs();

===== END SOURCE scripts/analyze-fuzzy-logs.js =====

===== BEGIN SOURCE scripts/build-ui-runtime.cjs | original-bytes=2491 | sha256=d06d143de5852e4d9c3a4d6fb24ab8e9fd9ea9657fd0f32196e92e3ecf2f1031 =====
#!/usr/bin/env node
/**
 * Build script that compiles and stages browser UI assets used by MCP tool pages. It centralizes bundling/runtime generation so UI resources are deterministic in local dev and CI.
 */
const path = require('path');
const fs = require('fs/promises');
const { build } = require('esbuild');

const target = process.argv[2];

const TARGETS = {
  'file-preview': {
    entry: 'src/ui/file-preview/src/main.ts',
    output: 'dist/ui/file-preview/preview-runtime.js',
    staticDir: 'src/ui/file-preview',
    styleLayers: [
      'src/ui/styles/base.css',
      'src/ui/styles/components/compact-row.css',
      'src/ui/styles/apps/file-preview.css'
    ]
  },
  'config-editor': {
    entry: 'src/ui/config-editor/src/main.ts',
    output: 'dist/ui/config-editor/config-editor-runtime.js',
    staticDir: 'src/ui/config-editor',
    styleLayers: [
      'src/ui/styles/base.css',
      'src/ui/styles/components/compact-row.css',
      'src/ui/styles/apps/config-editor.css'
    ]
  }
};

const STATIC_FILES = ['index.html'];
const projectRoot = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');

const selectedTargets =
  target
    ? [target]
    : Object.keys(TARGETS);

if (target && !TARGETS[target]) {
  console.error(
    `Usage: node scripts/build-ui-runtime.cjs [${Object.keys(TARGETS).join('|')}]`
  );
  process.exit(1);
}

async function buildTarget(targetName) {
  const { entry, output, staticDir, styleLayers } = TARGETS[targetName];
  const outputPath = path.join(projectRoot, output);
  const outputDir = path.dirname(outputPath);

  await fs.mkdir(outputDir, { recursive: true });

  for (const fileName of STATIC_FILES) {
    await fs.copyFile(
      path.join(projectRoot, staticDir, fileName),
      path.join(outputDir, fileName)
    );
  }

  const styles = await Promise.all(
    styleLayers.map((layerPath) => fs.readFile(path.join(projectRoot, layerPath), 'utf8'))
  );
  await fs.writeFile(path.join(outputDir, 'styles.css'), `${styles.join('\n\n')}\n`, 'utf8');

  await build({
    entryPoints: [path.join(projectRoot, entry)],
    bundle: true,
    format: 'iife',
    platform: 'browser',
    target: ['es2020'],
    outfile: outputPath,
    minify: true,
    sourcemap: false
  });
}

async function main() {
  for (const targetName of selectedTargets) {
    await buildTarget(targetName);
  }
}

main().catch((error) => {
  console.error(error);
  process.exit(1);
});

===== END SOURCE scripts/build-ui-runtime.cjs =====

===== BEGIN SOURCE scripts/check-context-acceptance.mjs | original-bytes=3148 | sha256=94dac530f7c13d9b5912f6f4543e784a304abeacedaa0197080fa036e530d547 =====
// Current implementation consistency check; not the archived planning-only gate.
import { readFileSync, existsSync } from 'node:fs';
import { resolve, dirname } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
const root = fileURLToPath(new URL('../', import.meta.url));
const read = path => readFileSync(resolve(root, path), 'utf8').replace(/^\uFEFF/, '');
const spec = read('specs/002-device-local-context/spec.md');
const tasks = read('specs/002-device-local-context/tasks.md');
const errors = [];
const requirements = [...spec.matchAll(/^- \*\*(FR-\d{3})\*\*/gm)].map(m => m[1]);
const criteria = [...spec.matchAll(/^- \*\*(SC-\d{3})\*\*/gm)].map(m => m[1]);
const ids = [...tasks.matchAll(/^- \[[ xX]\] (T\d{3})/gm)].map(m => m[1]);
if (requirements.length !== 32 || criteria.length !== 8 || ids.length !== 62) errors.push('Unexpected spec/task inventory');
ids.forEach((id, index) => { if (id !== `T${String(index + 1).padStart(3, '0')}`) errors.push(`Nonsequential ${id}`); });
for (const id of requirements) if (!tasks.includes(`| ${id} |`)) errors.push(`Unmapped ${id}`);
for (const id of criteria) if (!tasks.includes(`${id}:`)) errors.push(`Unmapped ${id}`);
for (const match of tasks.matchAll(/^\| FR-\d{3} \| ([^\n]+)\|$/gm)) {
  for (const id of match[1].match(/T\d{3}/g) ?? []) if (!ids.includes(id)) errors.push(`Unknown mapped task ${id}`);
}
const docs = ['spec.md', 'plan.md', 'research.md', 'data-model.md', 'integration-110.md', 'quickstart.md', 'tasks.md',
  'contracts/context-api.md', 'contracts/context-policy.md', 'contracts/skill-contract.md', 'checklists/requirements.md', 'checklists/implementation-gates.md']
  .map(file => `specs/002-device-local-context/${file}`)
  .concat(['README.md', 'PRIVACY.md', 'docs/context.md', 'skills/mcp-device-context/SKILL.md']);
for (const file of docs) {
  if (!existsSync(resolve(root, file))) { errors.push(`Missing ${file}`); continue; }
  const body = read(file);
  if (/\[NEEDS CLARIFICATION:|\[###-feature-name\]/.test(body)) errors.push(`Unfilled scaffold ${file}`);
  for (const match of body.matchAll(/\[[^\]\n]+\]\(([^)]+)\)/g)) {
    const target = match[1].split('#')[0];
    if (target && !/^[a-z]+:/i.test(target) && !existsSync(resolve(root, dirname(file), target))) errors.push(`Broken link ${file}: ${target}`);
  }
}
const pkg = JSON.parse(read('package.json')), lock = JSON.parse(read('package-lock.json'));
if (pkg.version !== '1.0.11' || lock.version !== pkg.version || lock.packages[''].version !== pkg.version) errors.push('Version identity mismatch');
if (pkg.engines.node !== '>=22.13.0' || lock.packages[''].engines.node !== pkg.engines.node) errors.push('Node floor mismatch');
if (!read('src/version.ts').includes("'1.0.11'")) errors.push('Runtime version mismatch');
console.log(JSON.stringify({ kind: 'implementation-consistency-not-independent-review', requirements: requirements.length,
  success_criteria: criteria.length, tasks: ids.length, checked_tasks: [...tasks.matchAll(/^- \[[xX]\] T/gm)].length,
  passed: errors.length === 0, errors }, null, 2));
process.exitCode = errors.length ? 1 : 0;

===== END SOURCE scripts/check-context-acceptance.mjs =====

===== BEGIN SOURCE scripts/clear-fuzzy-logs.js | original-bytes=1617 | sha256=c3f4c17868222627457ce1d8dab990e43ac2baa6f664a8442eb7f348691ebb3f =====
#!/usr/bin/env node

import { fuzzySearchLogger } from '../dist/utils/fuzzySearchLogger.js';

// Simple argument parsing
const args = process.argv.slice(2);
let skipConfirmation = false;

// Parse arguments
for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i++) {
  if (args[i] === '--yes' || args[i] === '-y') {
    skipConfirmation = true;
  }
}

if (args.includes('--help') || args.includes('-h')) {
  console.log(`Clear fuzzy search logs

Usage: node clear-fuzzy-logs.js [options]

Options:
  -y, --yes   Skip confirmation prompt
  -h, --help  Show this help message`);
  process.exit(0);
}

async function clearLogs() {
  try {
    const logPath = await fuzzySearchLogger.getLogPath();
    
    if (!skipConfirmation) {
      console.log(`About to clear fuzzy search logs at: ${logPath}`);
      console.log('This action cannot be undone. Continue? (y/N)');
      
      process.stdin.setRawMode(true);
      process.stdin.resume();
      
      const answer = await new Promise((resolve) => {
        process.stdin.on('data', (key) => {
          process.stdin.setRawMode(false);
          resolve(key.toString().toLowerCase());
        });
      });
      
      if (answer !== 'y') {
        console.log('Operation cancelled.');
        process.exit(0);
      }
    }
    
    await fuzzySearchLogger.clearLog();
    console.log(`✅ Fuzzy search logs cleared successfully.`);
    console.log(`Log file location: ${logPath}`);
    
  } catch (error) {
    console.error('Failed to clear fuzzy search logs:', error.message);
    process.exit(1);
  }
}

clearLogs();

===== BEGIN SOURCE scripts/copy-official-ca.cjs | original-bytes=743 | sha256=c5d10f5e336ce8b5e9325e1122708459bae96b8c30fd8a2b29d2980585b7c6bb =====
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const { X509Certificate } = require('crypto');

const root = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
const source = path.join(root, 'src', 'data', 'official-app-ca.pem');
const destination = path.join(root, 'dist', 'data', 'official-app-ca.pem');

fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(destination), { recursive: true });
if (!fs.existsSync(source)) {
  fs.rmSync(destination, { force: true });
  process.exit(0);
}

const pem = fs.readFileSync(source, 'utf8');
const certificate = new X509Certificate(pem);
if (!certificate.ca) throw new Error('official-app-ca.pem is not marked as a CA certificate');
fs.writeFileSync(destination, pem.endsWith('\n') ? pem : `${pem}\n`, { mode: 0o644 });

===== BEGIN SOURCE scripts/count-tokens.js | original-bytes=9092 | sha256=a59e71dd5c810458be18ae92e290a3bdaeecbcbfe409f1b63724564b5e079f5a =====
#!/usr/bin/env node
/**
 * Count tokens consumed by MCP Device tool definitions.
 * 
 * Spawns the MCP server, queries tools/list via JSON-RPC,
 * then uses js-tiktoken (cl100k_base) to count tokens per tool.
 * 
 * Usage: npm run count-tokens
 */

import { join, dirname } from 'path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'url';
import { spawn } from 'child_process';
import { encodingForModel } from 'js-tiktoken';

const __filename = fileURLToPath(import.meta.url);
const __dirname = dirname(__filename);
const rootDir = join(__dirname, '..');

const colors = {
  reset: '\x1b[0m',
  red: '\x1b[31m',
  green: '\x1b[32m',
  yellow: '\x1b[33m',
  blue: '\x1b[34m',
  cyan: '\x1b[36m',
  dim: '\x1b[2m',
  bold: '\x1b[1m',
};

function extractToolsFromServer() {
  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    const serverPath = join(rootDir, 'dist', 'index.js');
    const server = spawn('node', [serverPath], {
      stdio: ['pipe', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
      env: { ...process.env, HOME: process.env.HOME },
    });

    let output = '';
    const messages = [];

    server.stdout.on('data', (data) => {
      output += data.toString();
      const lines = output.split('\n');
      output = lines.pop() || '';
      for (const line of lines) {
        if (line.trim()) {
          try { messages.push(JSON.parse(line)); } catch {}
        }
      }
    });

    server.stderr.on('data', () => {}); // silence

    const initRequest = {
      jsonrpc: '2.0', id: 1, method: 'initialize',
      params: {
        protocolVersion: '2024-11-05',
        capabilities: {},
        clientInfo: { name: 'count-tokens', version: '1.0.0' },
      },
    };
    server.stdin.write(JSON.stringify(initRequest) + '\n');

    setTimeout(() => {
      const toolsRequest = {
        jsonrpc: '2.0', id: 2, method: 'tools/list', params: {},
      };
      server.stdin.write(JSON.stringify(toolsRequest) + '\n');

      setTimeout(() => {
        server.kill();
        const resp = messages.find((m) => m.id === 2 && m.result);
        if (!resp) return reject(new Error('No tools/list response'));
        resolve(resp.result.tools);
      }, 1500);
    }, 500);

    server.on('error', (e) => reject(e));
  });
}

function countTokens(enc, text) {
  return enc.encode(text).length;
}

function tokenizeToolDefinition(enc, tool) {
  // Approximate how an MCP client serializes a tool for the LLM context.
  // Most clients send the full JSON schema including name, description, inputSchema.
  const serialized = JSON.stringify(tool);
  return {
    total: countTokens(enc, serialized),
    name: countTokens(enc, tool.name || ''),
    description: countTokens(enc, tool.description || ''),
    schema: countTokens(enc, JSON.stringify(tool.inputSchema || {})),
  };
}

async function main() {
  const flag = process.argv[2]; // --json or --top or nothing
  const isJson = flag === '--json';

  if (!isJson) {
    console.log(`${colors.cyan}🔢 MCP Device — Token Counter${colors.reset}`);
    console.log(`${colors.dim}   Using cl100k_base tokenizer (cl100k_base approximation)${colors.reset}\n`);

    console.log(`${colors.dim}   Starting server and querying tools/list...${colors.reset}`);
  }

  const tools = await extractToolsFromServer();

  if (!isJson) {
    console.log(`${colors.green}   ✓ Retrieved ${tools.length} tools${colors.reset}\n`);
  }

  const enc = encodingForModel('gpt-4');

  const results = tools.map((tool) => {
    const tokens = tokenizeToolDefinition(enc, tool);
    return { toolName: String(tool.name || 'unknown'), ...tokens };
  });

  // Sort by total tokens descending
  results.sort((a, b) => b.total - a.total);

  const grandTotal = results.reduce((s, r) => s + r.total, 0);
  const descTotal = results.reduce((s, r) => s + r.description, 0);
  const schemaTotal = results.reduce((s, r) => s + r.schema, 0);

  if (!isJson) {
    // --- Summary ---
    console.log(`${colors.bold}═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════${colors.reset}`);
    console.log(`${colors.bold}  SUMMARY${colors.reset}`);
    console.log(`${colors.bold}═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════${colors.reset}`);
    console.log(`  Tools count:        ${colors.bold}${tools.length}${colors.reset}`);
    console.log(`  Total tokens:       ${colors.bold}${grandTotal.toLocaleString()}${colors.reset}`);
    console.log(`  ├─ Descriptions:    ${descTotal.toLocaleString()} (${pct(descTotal, grandTotal)})`);
    console.log(`  └─ Schemas:         ${schemaTotal.toLocaleString()} (${pct(schemaTotal, grandTotal)})`);

    console.log(`  Context window usage (200K): ${colors.yellow}${pct(grandTotal, 200000)}${colors.reset}`);
    console.log();

    // --- Per-tool table ---
    console.log(`${colors.bold}═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════${colors.reset}`);
    console.log(`${colors.bold}  PER-TOOL BREAKDOWN (sorted by total tokens)${colors.reset}`);
    console.log(`${colors.bold}═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════${colors.reset}`);
    console.log(
      `  ${'#'.padEnd(3)} ${'Tool Name'.padEnd(35)} ${'Total'.padStart(7)} ${'Desc'.padStart(7)} ${'Schema'.padStart(7)} ${'Bar'}`
    );
    console.log(`  ${'-'.repeat(75)}`);

    const maxTokens = results[0]?.total || 1;
    results.forEach((r, i) => {
      const barLen = Math.round((r.total / maxTokens) * 30);
      const bar = '█'.repeat(barLen) + '░'.repeat(30 - barLen);
      const color = r.total > 500 ? colors.yellow : colors.dim;
      console.log(
        `  ${String(i + 1).padEnd(3)} ${color}${r.toolName.padEnd(35)}${colors.reset} ${String(r.total).padStart(7)} ${String(r.description).padStart(7)} ${String(r.schema).padStart(7)} ${colors.dim}${bar}${colors.reset}`
      );
    });

    console.log();

    // --- Category breakdown ---
    const categories = categorize(results);
    console.log(`${colors.bold}═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════${colors.reset}`);
    console.log(`${colors.bold}  BY CATEGORY${colors.reset}`);
    console.log(`${colors.bold}═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════${colors.reset}`);

    for (const [cat, catResults] of Object.entries(categories)) {
      const catTotal = catResults.reduce((s, r) => s + r.total, 0);
      console.log(
        `  ${cat.padEnd(25)} ${String(catResults.length).padStart(3)} tools  ${String(catTotal).padStart(7)} tokens  (${pct(catTotal, grandTotal)})`
      );
    }
    console.log();
  }

  // --- JSON output ---
  if (isJson) {
    const output = {
      timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
      toolCount: tools.length,
      totalTokens: grandTotal,
      descriptionTokens: descTotal,
      schemaTokens: schemaTotal,
      contextWindowPct: (grandTotal / 200000 * 100).toFixed(2),
      tools: results,
    };
    console.log(JSON.stringify(output, null, 2));
  }

  // enc cleanup not needed for js-tiktoken
}

function pct(part, whole) {
  return `${((part / whole) * 100).toFixed(1)}%`;
}

function categorize(results) {
  const cats = {};
  for (const r of results) {
    let cat;
    if (r.toolName.startsWith('macos_ax_')) cat = 'macOS AX';
    else if (r.toolName.startsWith('electron_debug_')) cat = 'Electron Debug';
    else if (['read_file', 'read_multiple_files', 'write_file', 'write_pdf', 'edit_block', 'create_directory', 'move_file', 'list_directory', 'get_file_info'].includes(r.toolName)) cat = 'Filesystem';
    else if (['start_search', 'get_more_search_results', 'stop_search', 'list_searches'].includes(r.toolName)) cat = 'Search';
    else if (['start_process', 'interact_with_process', 'read_process_output', 'force_terminate', 'list_sessions'].includes(r.toolName)) cat = 'Process/Terminal';
    else if (['list_processes', 'kill_process'].includes(r.toolName)) cat = 'OS Processes';
    else if (['get_config', 'set_config_value'].includes(r.toolName)) cat = 'Configuration';
    else cat = 'Other';
    (cats[cat] ??= []).push(r);
  }
  return cats;
}

main().catch((e) => {
  console.error(`${colors.red}❌ ${e.message}${colors.reset}`);
  process.exit(1);
});

===== END SOURCE scripts/count-tokens.js =====

===== BEGIN SOURCE scripts/download-all-ripgrep.cjs | original-bytes=6736 | sha256=927848e034b6c3b39d6e56130aa613894aed43ca2d7096cd4ed83e037b54c0e4 =====
#!/usr/bin/env node

/**
 * Download all ripgrep binaries for cross-platform MCPB bundles
 * 
 * This script downloads ripgrep binaries for all supported platforms
 * and places them in node_modules/@vscode/ripgrep/bin/ with platform-specific names.
 */

const https = require('https');
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const { promisify } = require('util');
const yauzl = require('yauzl');
const { pipeline } = require('stream');

const streamPipeline = promisify(pipeline);

// Ripgrep versions from @vscode/ripgrep
const VERSION = 'v13.0.0-10';
const MULTI_ARCH_VERSION = 'v13.0.0-4';

// All supported platforms
const PLATFORMS = [
  { name: 'x86_64-apple-darwin', ext: 'tar.gz', version: VERSION },
  { name: 'aarch64-apple-darwin', ext: 'tar.gz', version: VERSION },
  { name: 'x86_64-pc-windows-msvc', ext: 'zip', version: VERSION },
  { name: 'aarch64-pc-windows-msvc', ext: 'zip', version: VERSION },
  { name: 'i686-pc-windows-msvc', ext: 'zip', version: VERSION },
  { name: 'x86_64-unknown-linux-musl', ext: 'tar.gz', version: VERSION },
  { name: 'aarch64-unknown-linux-musl', ext: 'tar.gz', version: VERSION },
  { name: 'i686-unknown-linux-musl', ext: 'tar.gz', version: VERSION },
  { name: 'arm-unknown-linux-gnueabihf', ext: 'tar.gz', version: MULTI_ARCH_VERSION },
  { name: 'powerpc64le-unknown-linux-gnu', ext: 'tar.gz', version: MULTI_ARCH_VERSION },
  { name: 's390x-unknown-linux-gnu', ext: 'tar.gz', version: VERSION }
];

const TEMP_DIR = path.join(__dirname, '../.ripgrep-downloads');
const OUTPUT_DIR = path.join(__dirname, '../node_modules/@vscode/ripgrep/bin');

function ensureDir(dir) {
  if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) {
    fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
  }
}

async function downloadFile(url, dest) {
  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    console.log(`  📥 ${path.basename(dest)}...`);
    
    https.get(url, { 
      headers: { 
        'User-Agent': 'vscode-ripgrep',
        'Accept': 'application/octet-stream'
      }
    }, (response) => {
      if (response.statusCode === 302 || response.statusCode === 301) {
        // Follow redirect
        downloadFile(response.headers.location, dest).then(resolve).catch(reject);
        return;
      }
      
      if (response.statusCode !== 200) {
        reject(new Error(`Failed to download: ${response.statusCode}`));
        return;
      }

      const file = fs.createWriteStream(dest);
      response.pipe(file);
      
      file.on('finish', () => {
        file.close();
        resolve();
      });
      
      file.on('error', (err) => {
        fs.unlink(dest, () => reject(err));
      });
    }).on('error', reject);
  });
}

async function extractZip(zipPath, outputDir, targetBinary) {
  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    yauzl.open(zipPath, { lazyEntries: true }, (err, zipfile) => {
      if (err) return reject(err);

      zipfile.readEntry();
      zipfile.on('entry', (entry) => {
        if (entry.fileName.endsWith('rg.exe')) {
          zipfile.openReadStream(entry, (err, readStream) => {
            if (err) return reject(err);

            const outputPath = path.join(outputDir, targetBinary);
            const writeStream = fs.createWriteStream(outputPath);
            
            readStream.pipe(writeStream);
            writeStream.on('close', () => {
              zipfile.close();
              resolve();
            });
            writeStream.on('error', reject);
          });
        } else {
          zipfile.readEntry();
        }
      });

      zipfile.on('end', resolve);
      zipfile.on('error', reject);
    });
  });
}

async function extractTarGz(tarPath, outputDir, targetBinary) {
  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    const { execSync } = require('child_process');
    const tempExtractDir = path.join(TEMP_DIR, 'extract-' + Date.now());
    
    try {
      ensureDir(tempExtractDir);
      
      // Extract tar.gz
      execSync(`tar -xzf "${tarPath}" -C "${tempExtractDir}"`, { stdio: 'pipe' });
      
      // Find the rg binary
      const files = fs.readdirSync(tempExtractDir, { recursive: true });
      const rgFile = files.find(f => f.endsWith('/rg') || f === 'rg');
      
      if (rgFile) {
        const sourcePath = path.join(tempExtractDir, rgFile);
        const destPath = path.join(outputDir, targetBinary);
        fs.copyFileSync(sourcePath, destPath);
        fs.chmodSync(destPath, 0o755);
      }
      
      // Cleanup
      fs.rmSync(tempExtractDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
      resolve();
    } catch (error) {
      reject(error);
    }
  });
}

async function downloadAndExtractPlatform(platform) {
  const { name, ext, version } = platform;
  const fileName = `ripgrep-${version}-${name}.${ext}`;
  const url = `https://github.com/microsoft/ripgrep-prebuilt/releases/download/${version}/${fileName}`;
  const tempFile = path.join(TEMP_DIR, fileName);
  
  // Determine output binary name
  const isWindows = name.includes('windows');
  const binaryName = isWindows ? `rg-${name}.exe` : `rg-${name}`;
  const outputPath = path.join(OUTPUT_DIR, binaryName);
  
  // Skip if binary already exists
  if (fs.existsSync(outputPath)) {
    console.log(`  ✓ ${name} (cached)`);
    return;
  }
  
  try {
    // Download if not already cached
    if (!fs.existsSync(tempFile)) {
      await downloadFile(url, tempFile);
    }
    
    // Extract
    ensureDir(OUTPUT_DIR);
    
    if (ext === 'zip') {
      await extractZip(tempFile, OUTPUT_DIR, binaryName);
    } else {
      await extractTarGz(tempFile, OUTPUT_DIR, binaryName);
    }
    
    console.log(`  ✓ ${name}`);
  } catch (error) {
    console.error(`  ✗ Failed: ${name} - ${error.message}`);
    throw error;
  }
}

async function main() {
  console.log('🌍 Downloading ripgrep binaries for all platforms...\n');
  
  ensureDir(TEMP_DIR);
  ensureDir(OUTPUT_DIR);
  
  // Download and extract all platforms
  for (const platform of PLATFORMS) {
    await downloadAndExtractPlatform(platform);
  }
  
  console.log('\n✅ All ripgrep binaries downloaded successfully!');
  console.log(`📁 Binaries location: ${OUTPUT_DIR}`);
  console.log('\nFiles created:');
  const files = fs.readdirSync(OUTPUT_DIR).filter(f => f.startsWith('rg-'));
  files.forEach(f => console.log(`   - ${f}`));
}

// Run if called directly
if (require.main === module) {
  main().catch((error) => {
    console.error('❌ Error:', error);
    process.exit(1);
  });
}

module.exports = { downloadAndExtractPlatform, PLATFORMS };

===== END SOURCE scripts/download-all-ripgrep.cjs =====
